Working in a Google Colab Enterprise notebook, I am trying to fine tune the TrOCR model using my own handwriting. I have a folder of cropped images of words in a folder named "word_crops". I have a CSV file with columns "crop_filename" and "recognized_text" where the first column is the path to an image and the second column is my label. Please guide me through the code cells necessary to fine tune and test the model

Laying the groundwork

I’ll guide the user through setting up their environment, loading data, preprocessing, defining, fine-tuning, saving, and testing the TrOCR model for handwriting recognition.

Certainly! Fine-tuning the TrOCR (Transformer-based Optical Character Recognition) model using your own handwriting data involves several steps, including setting up the environment, preparing your dataset, configuring the model, training, and evaluating its performance. Below is a comprehensive guide with code snippets tailored for a Google Colab Enterprise notebook.
1. Setup the Environment

First, ensure that all necessary libraries are installed and imported. We'll be using Hugging Face's transformers and datasets libraries, along with other essential packages.

# Install required packages
!pip install transformers datasets torch torchvision

# Verify GPU availability
import torch
device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'
print(f'Using device: {device}')

2. Import Necessary Libraries

import os
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel, Seq2SeqTrainingArguments, Seq2SeqTrainer
from datasets import Dataset, load_metric
import torch

3. Prepare the Dataset

Assuming your directory structure is as follows:

/content/
    word_crops/
        img1.png
        img2.png
        ...
    labels.csv

Let's load the CSV and prepare the dataset.

# Define paths
images_dir = '/content/word_crops'
labels_file = '/content/labels.csv'

# Load the CSV file
df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Display first few entries
df.head()

Ensure that your CSV has the columns crop_filename (image path) and recognized_text (labels).
4. Create a Custom Dataset

We'll create a Hugging Face Dataset object to facilitate the training process.

# Create full image paths
df['image_path'] = df['crop_filename'].apply(lambda x: os.path.join(images_dir, x))

# Select relevant columns
df = df[['image_path', 'recognized_text']]

# Convert pandas DataFrame to Hugging Face Dataset
dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(df)

# Display a sample
print(dataset[0])

5. Initialize the Processor and Model

We'll use the TrOCRProcessor for preprocessing and the pre-trained TrOCR model as a starting point.

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

6. Define Preprocessing Function

This function will handle image loading and encoding the labels.

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    # Tokenize labels
    inputs = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    labels = processor.tokenizer(examples['recognized_text'], padding="max_length", truncation=True, max_length=32).input_ids
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    return {"pixel_values": inputs, "labels": labels}

7. Apply Preprocessing

Apply the preprocessing function to the dataset.

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(preprocess_function, batched=True, remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"])

8. Split the Dataset

It's good practice to have separate training and validation sets.

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

9. Define Training Arguments

Configure the training parameters such as learning rate, batch size, and number of epochs.

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    push_to_hub=False,
)

10. Define Evaluation Metric

We'll use the Word Error Rate (WER) as our evaluation metric.

# Load WER metric
wer_metric = load_metric("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    # Decode predictions and labels
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    return {"wer": wer}

11. Initialize the Trainer

Set up the Seq2SeqTrainer with the model, arguments, and datasets.

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor.feature_extractor,  # Not strictly necessary here
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

12. Start Training

Begin the fine-tuning process. This step might take some time depending on your dataset size and hardware.

trainer.train()

    Note: Ensure that your Colab runtime has sufficient GPU resources. If you encounter memory issues, consider reducing the per_device_train_batch_size or using gradient accumulation.

13. Save the Fine-Tuned Model

After training, save the model and processor for future use.

# Save the model and processor
trainer.save_model("./trocr-finetuned")
processor.save_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")

14. Testing the Model

Let's test the fine-tuned model on some sample images to see how well it performs.

# Reload the fine-tuned model and processor
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

# Function to predict text from image
def predict(image_path):
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    pixel_values = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    pixel_values = pixel_values.to(device)
    
    generated_ids = model.generate(pixel_values)
    generated_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]
    return generated_text

# Test on a few samples from the validation set
import random

sample_indices = random.sample(range(len(eval_dataset)), 5)
for idx in sample_indices:
    sample = eval_dataset[idx]
    image_path = df['image_path'][idx]
    true_text = df['recognized_text'][idx]
    predicted_text = predict(image_path)
    print(f"Image: {image_path}")
    print(f"True Text: {true_text}")
    print(f"Predicted Text: {predicted_text}\n")

15. Additional Evaluation (Optional)

For a more comprehensive evaluation, you can compute the overall WER on the validation set.

# Evaluate on the validation set
eval_results = trainer.evaluate()

print(f"Validation WER: {eval_results['wer']}")

16. Tips and Best Practices

    Data Quality: Ensure that your images are of good quality and labels are accurate. Clean data significantly improves model performance.

    Augmentation: Consider applying data augmentation techniques (e.g., rotation, scaling) to make the model more robust to variations in handwriting.

    Hyperparameter Tuning: Experiment with different learning rates, batch sizes, and number of epochs to find the optimal configuration for your dataset.

    Regular Checkpoints: Save checkpoints at regular intervals to prevent loss of progress in case of interruptions.

    Monitoring: Utilize tools like TensorBoard for monitoring training metrics in real-time.

17. Troubleshooting

    Out of Memory Errors: If you encounter GPU memory issues, try reducing the per_device_train_batch_size or enable gradient checkpointing.

    Slow Training: Ensure that your Colab runtime is using a GPU. You can check this by going to Runtime > Change runtime type and selecting GPU.

    Low Performance: If the model isn't performing as expected, consider increasing the dataset size, improving data quality, or further fine-tuning the hyperparameters.

By following the steps above, you should be able to fine-tune the TrOCR model on your own handwriting dataset effectively. Feel free to adjust the parameters and configurations to better suit your specific needs and dataset characteristics.

3. Prepare the Dataset

4. Create a Custom Dataset

5. Initialize the Processor and Model

We'll use the TrOCRProcessor for preprocessing and the pre-trained TrOCR model as a starting point.

from transformers import logging

# Suppress warnings and info logs
logging.set_verbosity_error()

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

6. Define Preprocessing Function

This function will handle image loading and encoding the labels. We'll use the TrOCRProcessor to preprocess the images and tokenize the labels.

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(examples['recognized_text'], padding="max_length", truncation=True, max_length=32).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [[(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq] for label_seq in labels]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

Explanation:

    Images: Loaded and converted to RGB.
    Pixel Values: Processed using the TrOCRProcessor.
    Labels: Tokenized with padding and truncation. Padding tokens are replaced with -100 to be ignored during loss computation.

7. Apply Preprocessing

Apply the preprocessing function to the dataset.

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(preprocess_function, batched=True, remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"])

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

Sample Output:

{'pixel_values': tensor([...]), 'labels': tensor([...])}

8. Split the Dataset

It's good practice to have separate training and validation sets.

# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

9. Define Training Arguments

Configure the training parameters such as learning rate, batch size, and number of epochs.

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
)

Key Parameters:

    output_dir: Directory to save model checkpoints.
    evaluation_strategy: Frequency of evaluation (e.g., every eval_steps).
    learning_rate: Initial learning rate for training.
    per_device_train_batch_size & per_device_eval_batch_size: Batch sizes.
    num_train_epochs: Number of training epochs.
    fp16: Enable mixed precision training for faster computation.
    load_best_model_at_end: Automatically load the best model at the end of training based on the evaluation metric.
    metric_for_best_model: The metric to use to compare models.

10. Define Evaluation Metric

We'll use the Word Error Rate (WER) as our evaluation metric.

# Load WER metric
wer_metric = load_metric("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

Explanation:

    Decoding: Converts token IDs back to text.
    Handling -100: Replaces -100 with the tokenizer's pad token ID before decoding.
    WER Calculation: Compares the predicted text with the reference labels.

11. Initialize the Trainer

Set up the Seq2SeqTrainer with the model, arguments, datasets, and metrics.

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

Note: The tokenizer parameter is set to the TrOCRProcessor, which contains both the feature extractor and tokenizer.
12. Start Training

Begin the fine-tuning process. This step might take some time depending on your dataset size and hardware.

trainer.train()

Pro Tips:

    Monitoring Progress: You can monitor training progress through the logs printed in the notebook.
    Early Stopping: Consider implementing early stopping to prevent overfitting if your dataset is large enough.

16. Tips and Best Practices

    Data Quality: Ensure that your images are clear and labels are accurate. Blurry or mislabeled data can hinder model performance.

    Data Augmentation: Apply techniques such as rotation, scaling, and adding noise to make the model robust to variations in handwriting.

from torchvision import transforms

augmentation_transforms = transforms.Compose([
    transforms.RandomRotation(degrees=10),
    transforms.RandomAffine(degrees=0, translate=(0.05, 0.05)),
    transforms.ColorJitter(brightness=0.1, contrast=0.1),
])

def augment_images(examples):
    augmented_images = []
    for image_path in examples['image_path']:
        image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        image = augmentation_transforms(image)
        augmented_images.append(image)
    examples['pixel_values'] = processor(images=augmented_images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    return examples

processed_dataset = dataset.map(augment_images, batched=True)

Hyperparameter Tuning: Experiment with different learning rates, batch sizes, and epochs to find the optimal configuration.

Regular Checkpoints: Save checkpoints at regular intervals to prevent loss of progress in case of interruptions.

            Experiment with different learning rates.

    Unexpected Tokenization Issues:

ImportError: cannot import name 'load_metric' from 'datasets' (/usr/local/lib/python3.10/dist-packages/datasets/__init__.py)

occurs because the load_metric function has been deprecated in the Hugging Face datasets library and has been moved to a separate library named evaluate. To resolve this issue, you'll need to:

    Install the evaluate library.
    Modify your import statements and metric loading accordingly.

    labels = [[(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq] for label_seq in labels]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

4.7 Apply Preprocessing

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(preprocess_function, batched=True, remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"])

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

4.8 Split the Dataset

# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

4.9 Define Training Arguments

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
)

4.10 Define Evaluation Metric

# Load WER metric using the 'evaluate' library
wer_metric = evaluate.load("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

4.11 Initialize the Trainer

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

4.12 Start Training

trainer.train()

Note: Training might take some time depending on your dataset size and hardware. Monitor the progress through the logs.
4.13 Save the Fine-Tuned Model

# Save the model and processor
trainer.save_model("./trocr-finetuned")
processor.save_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")

4.14 Testing the Model

# Reload the fine-tuned model and processor

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

# Function to predict text from image
def predict(image_path):
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    pixel_values = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    pixel_values = pixel_values.to(device)
    
    generated_ids = model.generate(pixel_values)
    generated_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]
    return generated_text

# Test on a few samples from the validation set
sample_indices = random.sample(range(len(eval_dataset)), 5)
for idx in sample_indices:
    sample = eval_dataset[idx]
    image_path = df['image_path'][idx]
    true_text = df['recognized_text'][idx]
    predicted_text = predict(image_path)
    print(f"Image: {image_path}")
    print(f"True Text: {true_text}")
    print(f"Predicted Text: {predicted_text}\n")

4.15 Additional Evaluation (Optional)

# Evaluate on the validation set
eval_results = trainer.evaluate()

print(f"Validation WER: {eval_results['wer']}")

5. Additional Recommendations
5.1 Data Augmentation (Optional but Recommended)

To enhance model robustness, consider applying data augmentation techniques such as rotation, scaling, and adding noise.

from torchvision import transforms

augmentation_transforms = transforms.Compose([
    transforms.RandomRotation(degrees=10),
    transforms.RandomAffine(degrees=0, translate=(0.05, 0.05)),
    transforms.ColorJitter(brightness=0.1, contrast=0.1),
])

def augment_images(examples):
    augmented_images = []
    for image_path in examples['image_path']:
        image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        image = augmentation_transforms(image)
        augmented_images.append(image)
    examples['pixel_values'] = processor(images=augmented_images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    return examples

processed_dataset = dataset.map(augment_images, batched=True)

        learning_rate: Initial learning rate for training.

        num_train_epochs: Number of training epochs.

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them

    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

    Explanation:

Apologies for the inconvenience you're encountering. The prompt for a Weights & Biases (wandb) API key typically arises because the Hugging Face Trainer is attempting to log training metrics to wandb by default. If you prefer not to use wandb for logging, you can easily disable this behavior by modifying the training arguments.

Below, I'll guide you through the necessary steps to disable wandb logging and proceed with fine-tuning your TrOCR model without being prompted for a wandb API key.
1. Understanding the Issue

When using Hugging Face's Trainer (or Seq2SeqTrainer), it integrates with various logging and tracking tools by default, including wandb. If wandb is installed in your environment, the trainer will attempt to log metrics to it, prompting you for an API key if you're not authenticated.

Objective: Prevent the trainer from attempting to use wandb, thereby eliminating the prompt for an API key.
2. Disabling wandb Logging

To disable wandb (or any other unwanted logging integrations), you can set the report_to parameter in the Seq2SeqTrainingArguments to ["none"]. This tells the trainer not to report metrics to any external logging services.
2.1 Update the Training Arguments

Locate the section in your code where you define the training arguments. Modify it to include the report_to parameter set to ["none"].

Updated Code for Step 4.9: Define Training Arguments

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
    report_to=["none"],  # Disable all logging integrations, including wandb
)

2.2 Explanation of Changes

    report_to=["none"]: This parameter disables all external logging integrations, including wandb, TensorBoard, and others. The trainer will only log metrics to the console.

    Note: If you wish to log to a specific service (e.g., TensorBoard) while disabling others like wandb, you can set report_to to include only the desired services. For example:

    report_to=["tensorboard"]  # Enables only TensorBoard logging

    However, for completely disabling all external logging (as per your requirement), use ["none"].

3. Complete Revised Code Incorporating Changes

For clarity, here's the complete revised code from the relevant sections, incorporating the changes to disable wandb logging.
3.1 Setup the Environment

Ensure all required packages, including jiwer, are installed.

# Install required packages, including jiwer
!pip install --upgrade transformers datasets torch torchvision evaluate jiwer

3.2 Import Necessary Libraries

import os
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from transformers import (
    TrOCRProcessor,
    VisionEncoderDecoderModel,
    Seq2SeqTrainingArguments,
    Seq2SeqTrainer
)
from datasets import Dataset
import evaluate  # Updated import
import torch
import numpy as np
import random

3.3 Prepare the Dataset

(Assuming your directory structure is already correctly set up.)

# Define paths
images_dir = '/content/word_crops'
labels_file = '/content/recognized_words_labeled.csv'

# Load the CSV file
df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Display first few entries to verify
df.head()

3.4 Create a Custom Dataset

# Create full image paths
df['image_path'] = df['crop_filename'].apply(lambda x: os.path.join(images_dir, x))

# Select relevant columns
df = df[['image_path', 'recognized_text']]

# Convert pandas DataFrame to Hugging Face Dataset
dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(df)

# Display a sample
print(dataset[0])

3.5 Initialize the Processor and Model

from transformers import logging

# Suppress warnings and info logs
logging.set_verbosity_error()

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

3.6 Define Preprocessing Function

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

3.7 Apply Preprocessing

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(
    preprocess_function,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"]
)

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

3.8 Split the Dataset

# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

3.9 Define Training Arguments

(Updated to disable wandb logging.)

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
    report_to=["none"],  # Disable all logging integrations, including wandb
)

3.10 Define Evaluation Metric

# Load WER metric using the 'evaluate' library
wer_metric = evaluate.load("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

3.11 Initialize the Trainer

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

3.12 Start Training

trainer.train()

    Pro Tips:

        Monitoring Progress: Metrics will be logged to the console without attempting to send them to wandb.
        Early Stopping: If desired, you can implement early stopping by adding the EarlyStoppingCallback to the trainer.

3.13 Save the Fine-Tuned Model

# Save the model and processor
trainer.save_model("./trocr-finetuned")
processor.save_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")

3.14 Testing the Model

# Reload the fine-tuned model and processor
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

# Function to predict text from image
def predict(image_path):
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    pixel_values = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    pixel_values = pixel_values.to(device)
    
    generated_ids = model.generate(pixel_values)
    generated_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]
    return generated_text

# Test on a few samples from the validation set
sample_indices = random.sample(range(len(eval_dataset)), 5)
for idx in sample_indices:
    sample = eval_dataset[idx]
    image_path = df['image_path'][idx]
    true_text = df['recognized_text'][idx]
    predicted_text = predict(image_path)
    print(f"Image: {image_path}")
    print(f"True Text: {true_text}")
    print(f"Predicted Text: {predicted_text}\n")

3.15 Additional Evaluation (Optional)

# Evaluate on the validation set
eval_results = trainer.evaluate()

print(f"Validation WER: {eval_results['wer']}")

4. Additional Recommendations
4.1 Verify wandb is Disabled

After making the above changes, ensure that wandb is indeed disabled:

    No wandb Prompts: The training process should no longer prompt you for a wandb API key.
    Logging Output: Metrics should appear directly in the notebook's output cells without attempting to send them to wandb.

4.2 Optional: Remove wandb Installation

If you want to ensure that wandb is completely disabled, you can uninstall it from your environment. However, this step is optional and not necessary if you've correctly set report_to=["none"].

# Uninstall wandb if it's installed
!pip uninstall -y wandb

    Caution: Uninstalling wandb will prevent any future use of wandb in your current Colab session. Only do this if you're certain you won't need it.

4.3 Enable Alternative Logging (Optional)

If you still want to log metrics but prefer not to use wandb, consider enabling TensorBoard or another supported service.

Example: Enable TensorBoard Logging

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    ...
    report_to=["tensorboard"],  # Enable only TensorBoard logging
    ...
)

Start TensorBoard:

    # Start TensorBoard

This setup allows you to monitor training metrics in real-time using TensorBoard without involving wandb.
5. Final Remarks

By adjusting the report_to parameter in your Seq2SeqTrainingArguments, you've successfully disabled wandb logging, preventing the prompt for a wandb API key. This allows you to proceed with fine-tuning your TrOCR model seamlessly.
Key Takeaways:

    report_to=["none"]: Disables all external logging integrations, including wandb.
    Alternative Logging: You can enable other logging services like TensorBoard by specifying them in the report_to list.
    Clean Environment: Optionally uninstall wandb to ensure it's not present in your environment.

Troubleshooting:

5.1. Pin Library Versions for Reproducibility

To prevent similar issues in the future, it's good practice to pin your library versions. This ensures that your environment remains consistent across different sessions.

# Create a requirements.txt file with pinned versions
with open("requirements.txt", "w") as f:
    f.write("transformers==4.35.0\n")
    f.write("datasets==2.10.0\n")  # Replace with your dataset version
    f.write("torch==2.0.0\n")       # Replace with your torch version
    f.write("torchvision==0.15.1\n")# Replace with your torchvision version
    f.write("evaluate==0.4.0\n")    # Replace with your evaluate version
    f.write("jiwer==4.3.1\n")        # Replace with your jiwer version

    Usage:

        Install all dependencies at once using the requirements.txt.
        This can be particularly useful if you share your notebook or replicate the environment elsewhere.

!pip install -r requirements.txt

5.2. Monitor Training Progress

Even though wandb is disabled, you can still monitor training progress using built-in logging or alternative tools like TensorBoard.

Enable TensorBoard Logging:

    Modify Training Arguments to Include TensorBoard:

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    ...
    report_to=["tensorboard"],  # Enable only TensorBoard logging
    ...
)

Install and Launch TensorBoard:

    # Install TensorBoard
    !pip install tensorboard

    # Load TensorBoard extension
    %load_ext tensorboard

    # Start TensorBoard
    %tensorboard --logdir=./trocr-finetuned/runs

    Note: Ensure that output_dir in training_args is set appropriately to capture TensorBoard logs.

5.3. Implement Early Stopping (Optional)

To prevent overfitting and save computational resources, consider implementing early stopping based on the evaluation metric.

from transformers import EarlyStoppingCallback

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
    callbacks=[EarlyStoppingCallback(early_stopping_patience=3)],  # Stops training if no improvement in 3 evaluations
)

    Note: Adjust early_stopping_patience based on your dataset and training dynamics.

5.4. Data Augmentation (Optional but Recommended)

Enhancing your dataset with augmented images can improve model robustness.

from torchvision import transforms

augmentation_transforms = transforms.Compose([
    transforms.RandomRotation(degrees=10),
    transforms.RandomAffine(degrees=0, translate=(0.05, 0.05)),
    transforms.ColorJitter(brightness=0.1, contrast=0.1),
])

def augment_images(examples):
    augmented_images = []
    for image_path in examples['image_path']:
        image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
        image = augmentation_transforms(image)
        augmented_images.append(image)
    examples['pixel_values'] = processor(images=augmented_images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    return examples

processed_dataset = dataset.map(augment_images, batched=True)

    Caution: Ensure that augmentations preserve the legibility of handwriting.

6. Troubleshooting
6.1. Still Encountering Errors After Downgrading

Possible Causes:

    Incomplete Downgrade: Ensure that the transformers library was successfully downgraded.
    Conflicting Dependencies: Other libraries (e.g., datasets, torch) might have compatibility issues with the downgraded transformers.

Solutions:

    Re-verify transformers Version:

import transformers
print(transformers.__version__)

Ensure All Dependencies Are Compatible:

    Refer to the Hugging Face transformers documentation for compatible versions of dependencies.
    Consider downgrading other libraries if necessary.

Clear and Reinstall Libraries:

    Uninstall all relevant libraries and reinstall the compatible versions.

    !pip uninstall -y transformers datasets torch torchvision evaluate jiwer
    !pip install transformers==4.35.0 datasets==2.10.0 torch==2.0.0 torchvision==0.15.1 evaluate==0.4.0 jiwer==4.3.1

    Restart the Runtime:
        After reinstallation, restart the runtime to apply changes.

6.2. GPU Not Recognized

Symptoms:

    device prints as cpu.
    Training is significantly slower.

Solutions:

    Enable GPU in Colab:
        Go to Runtime > Change runtime type.
        Select GPU as the hardware accelerator.
        Click Save.

    Verify GPU Access:

import torch
print(torch.cuda.is_available())
print(torch.cuda.current_device())
print(torch.cuda.get_device_name(0))

Expected Output:

True
0
NVIDIA Tesla T4  # or another GPU model

Reinstall CUDA-Compatible PyTorch:

Ensure that the installed PyTorch version is compatible with the GPU.

    !pip install torch torchvision torchaudio --extra-index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu118

    (Adjust the CUDA version (cu118) based on your environment.)

    Restart the Runtime:
        After making changes, restart the runtime.

6.3. Low Model Performance (High WER)

Possible Causes:

    Insufficient Training: Not enough epochs or inadequate learning rate.
    Data Quality Issues: Blurry images, inconsistent labels.
    Overfitting or Underfitting: Model complexity vs. dataset size.

Solutions:

    Increase Training Epochs:
        Consider increasing num_train_epochs if the model hasn't converged.

    Adjust Learning Rate:
        Experiment with different learning rates (e.g., 1e-5, 3e-5).

    Improve Data Quality:
        Ensure images are clear and labels are accurate.
        Remove or correct mislabeled samples.

    Enhance Data Diversity:
        Apply data augmentation to introduce variability.

    Implement Regularization:
        Use techniques like dropout or weight decay to prevent overfitting.

    Review Model Architecture:
        Ensure that the model is appropriate for your specific task and data.

6.4. Other Common Issues

Issue: Mismatch Between Image Paths and Labels

    Solution: Verify that each crop_filename in your CSV correctly corresponds to its label.

Issue: Tokenization Errors

    Solution: Ensure that labels do not contain unexpected special characters or exceed max_length.

Issue: Unexpected Runtime Errors

    Solution: Review error messages carefully, ensure all dependencies are correctly installed, and consult Hugging Face forums or GitHub issues for similar problems.

7. Final Remarks

By downgrading the transformers library to a stable earlier version and ensuring that all dependencies are compatible, you should be able to bypass the TypeError related to ViTModel.forward(). This adjustment allows you to continue fine-tuning the TrOCR model without interruptions.
Key Takeaways:

    Library Version Compatibility: Always ensure that the versions of your libraries are compatible, especially when dealing with rapidly evolving libraries like Hugging Face's transformers.

    Environment Management: Pinning library versions and managing dependencies meticulously can prevent many common issues.

    Proactive Monitoring: Regularly monitor library updates and known issues to stay ahead of potential problems.

Tracing the challenge

The user is working through fine-tuning the TrOCR model, encountering import and type errors, and resolving them by installing specific packages and versions. Now, a ValueError highlights a missing decoder_start_token_id attribute in the model's configuration, essential for token generation starting point.

Setting the configuration

To resolve the missing decoder_start_token_id, ensure the model's configuration includes it. Setting it to the tokenizer's decoder start token id, possibly eos_token_id or pad_token_id, might be necessary.

Identifying the token

For TrOCR models, setting the decoder_start_token_id involves pinpointing a specific token from the tokenizer, possibly halting a translation or padding token, and updating the model's configuration accurately.

Setting up the environment

To resolve the decoder_start_token_id error, ensure the model's configuration includes the correct vocab_size. Set the decoder_start_token_id to the tokenizer's decoder start token, possibly the eos_token_id.

Ensuring compatibility

Recommending a specific transformers version for model compatibility and setting decoder_start_token_id. Provide code to set and verify special tokens within the tokenizer.

I'm sorry to hear you're encountering another issue during the training process. The error:

ValueError: Make sure to set the decoder_start_token_id attribute of the model's configuration.

indicates that the model's configuration is missing the decoder_start_token_id attribute, which is essential for the decoder to know where to begin generating text. This is a common requirement for sequence-to-sequence models like TrOCR.

To resolve this, you'll need to explicitly set the decoder_start_token_id in the model's configuration. Below are the detailed steps to fix this issue and ensure a smooth training process.
1. Understanding the Error

    decoder_start_token_id: This attribute tells the decoder where to start generating text. Without it, the model doesn't know which token to use as the starting point for generation.

    Cause of the Error: The decoder_start_token_id is either not set or incorrectly set in the model's configuration.

2. Setting decoder_start_token_id

To set the decoder_start_token_id, follow these steps:
2.1. Inspect the Tokenizer's Special Tokens

First, identify which special token you can use as the decoder_start_token_id. Common choices include:

    eos_token_id (End of Sequence): Often used to indicate the end of a sequence.
    pad_token_id (Padding): Used for padding sequences to a uniform length.
    bos_token_id (Beginning of Sequence): Indicates the start of a sequence (if available).

Note: The choice depends on how the tokenizer is configured. For TrOCR, using eos_token_id is typically appropriate.

# Inspect the tokenizer's special tokens
print("Special Tokens:", processor.tokenizer.special_tokens_map)

Sample Output:

Special Tokens: {'eos_token': '</s>', 'bos_token': '<s>', 'unk_token': '<unk>'}

2.2. Set decoder_start_token_id in the Model's Configuration

Based on the special tokens available, set the decoder_start_token_id. For TrOCR, using the eos_token_id is standard.

# Check if decoder_start_token_id is already set
if model.config.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    # Set it to the eos_token_id
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
else:
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id is already set to: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")

Explanation:

    Check: Determines if decoder_start_token_id is already set.
    Set: If not set, assigns it to the eos_token_id from the tokenizer.

2.3. Verify the Setting

Ensure that the decoder_start_token_id is correctly set.

print("Decoder Start Token ID:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)
print("Decoder Start Token:", processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.decoder_start_token_id))

Sample Output:

Decoder Start Token ID: 2
Decoder Start Token: </s>

3. Complete Revised Workflow Incorporating the Fix

For clarity, here's the complete revised workflow, including the steps to set the decoder_start_token_id.
3.1. Setup the Environment

Ensure all required packages are installed with compatible versions.

# Install specific transformers version and other required packages
!pip install transformers==4.37.1 datasets==2.10.0 evaluate==0.4.0 jiwer==4.3.1

3.2. Import Necessary Libraries

import os
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from transformers import (
    TrOCRProcessor,
    VisionEncoderDecoderModel,
    Seq2SeqTrainingArguments,
    Seq2SeqTrainer
)
from datasets import Dataset
import evaluate
import torch
import numpy as np
import random

3.3. Verify GPU Availability

device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'
print(f'Using device: {device}')

Expected Output:

Using device: cuda

(If cpu, enable GPU via Runtime > Change runtime type > GPU and restart the runtime.)
3.4. Prepare the Dataset

Assuming your directory structure:

/content/
    word_crops/
        img1.png
        img2.png
        ...
    recognized_words_labeled.csv

# Define paths
images_dir = '/content/word_crops'
labels_file = '/content/recognized_words_labeled.csv'

# Load the CSV file
df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Display first few entries to verify
df.head()

3.5. Create a Custom Dataset

# Create full image paths
df['image_path'] = df['crop_filename'].apply(lambda x: os.path.join(images_dir, x))

# Select relevant columns
df = df[['image_path', 'recognized_text']]

# Convert pandas DataFrame to Hugging Face Dataset
dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(df)

# Display a sample
print(dataset[0])

Sample Output:

{'image_path': '/content/word_crops/img1.png', 'recognized_text': 'hello'}

3.6. Initialize the Processor and Model

from transformers import logging

# Suppress warnings and info logs
logging.set_verbosity_error()

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

3.7. Define Preprocessing Function

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

3.8. Apply Preprocessing

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(
    preprocess_function,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"]
)

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

Sample Output:

{'pixel_values': tensor([...]), 'labels': tensor([...])}

3.9. Split the Dataset

# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

Sample Output:

Number of training samples: 900
Number of validation samples: 100

(Adjust based on your actual dataset size.)
3.10. Define Training Arguments

Important: Disable wandb logging by setting report_to=["none"] and set the decoder_start_token_id.

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
    report_to=["none"],  # Disable all logging integrations, including wandb
)

3.11. Define Evaluation Metric

# Load WER metric using the 'evaluate' library
wer_metric = evaluate.load("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

3.12. Set decoder_start_token_id

This is the crucial step to fix the ValueError you're encountering.

# Check if decoder_start_token_id is set; if not, set it to eos_token_id
if model.config.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
else:
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id is already set to: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")

Expected Output:

decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: 2

Explanation:

    Check: Verifies if decoder_start_token_id is already set.
    Set: Assigns it to the eos_token_id from the tokenizer if not set.

print("Decoder Start Token ID:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)
print("Decoder Start Token:", processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.decoder_start_token_id))

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(

3.17. Testing the Model

Evaluate the fine-tuned model on sample images.

# Reload the fine-tuned model and processor
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

# Function to predict text from image
def predict(image_path):
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    pixel_values = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    pixel_values = pixel_values.to(device)
    
    generated_ids = model.generate(pixel_values)
    generated_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]
    return generated_text

# Test on a few samples from the validation set
sample_indices = random.sample(range(len(eval_dataset)), 5)
for idx in sample_indices:
    sample = eval_dataset[idx]
    image_path = df['image_path'][idx]
    true_text = df['recognized_text'][idx]
    predicted_text = predict(image_path)
    print(f"Image: {image_path}")
    print(f"True Text: {true_text}")
    print(f"Predicted Text: {predicted_text}\n")

Predicted Text: exampl

True Text: handwriting
Predicted Text: handwriting

...

Compute the overall WER on the validation set.

# Evaluate on the validation set
eval_results = trainer.evaluate()

4. Additional Recommendations
4.1. Pin Library Versions for Reproducibility

To prevent similar issues in the future, it's good practice to pin your library versions using a requirements.txt file.

# Create a requirements.txt file with pinned versions
with open("requirements.txt", "w") as f:
    f.write("transformers==4.37.1\n")
    f.write("datasets==2.10.0\n")
    f.write("evaluate==0.4.0\n")
    f.write("jiwer==4.3.1\n")

Usage:

!pip install -r requirements.txt

4.2. Monitor Training Progress with TensorBoard (Optional)

If you wish to have more structured logging, consider enabling TensorBoard.

    Modify Training Arguments:

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    ...
    report_to=["tensorboard"],  # Enable only TensorBoard logging
    ...
)

Install and Launch TensorBoard:

    # Install TensorBoard
    !pip install tensorboard

    # Load TensorBoard extension
    %load_ext tensorboard

    # Start TensorBoard
    %tensorboard --logdir=./trocr-finetuned/runs

4.3. Implement Early Stopping (Optional)

To prevent overfitting and save computational resources, implement early stopping based on the evaluation metric.

from transformers import EarlyStoppingCallback

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
    callbacks=[EarlyStoppingCallback(early_stopping_patience=3)],  # Stops training if no improvement in 3 evaluations
)

5. Troubleshooting
5.1. If You Still Encounter the decoder_start_token_id Error

    Double-Check the Setting:

    Ensure that the decoder_start_token_id is correctly set before initializing the trainer.

print("Decoder Start Token ID:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)
print("Decoder Start Token:", processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.decoder_start_token_id))

Expected Output:

    Decoder Start Token ID: 2
    Decoder Start Token: </s>

    Ensure No Overwrites:

    Verify that no other parts of your code are inadvertently modifying the model's configuration after setting decoder_start_token_id.

    Check Model Initialization Order:

    Make sure that you set decoder_start_token_id after loading the model but before initializing the trainer.

5.2. Additional Common Issues

    Mismatch Between Tokenizer and Model:

    Ensure that the tokenizer and model are compatible and come from the same pre-trained checkpoint.

assert processor.tokenizer.vocab_size == model.config.vocab_size, "Tokenizer and model vocab sizes do not match."

GPU Memory Issues:

If you encounter memory-related errors during training:

    Reduce Batch Size:

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    ...
    per_device_train_batch_size=8,  # Reduced from 16
    per_device_eval_batch_size=8,
    ...
)

Enable Gradient Accumulation:

    training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
        ...
        gradient_accumulation_steps=2,
        ...
    )

    Use Mixed Precision (Already Enabled with fp16=True):

    Ensure that your hardware supports mixed precision training.

Data Preprocessing Errors:

Ensure that all images are correctly loaded and that the labels do not contain unexpected characters or exceed the max_length.

    # Example: Verify label lengths
    max_label_length = df['recognized_text'].str.len().max()
    print(f"Maximum label length: {max_label_length}")

    Adjust max_length in the tokenizer accordingly.

6. Final Remarks

    Set decoder_start_token_id: Assign the appropriate token ID to the model's configuration.
    Verify the Setting: Ensure that the attribute is correctly set.
    Proceed with Training: Initialize the trainer and start the training process without encountering wandb prompts or configuration errors.

Remember to maintain consistency in library versions and monitor the training process to ensure optimal performance. If you encounter any further issues or have additional questions, feel free to ask!

I'm sorry to hear you're encountering another issue during your training process. The error:

ValueError: Make sure to set the pad_token_id attribute of the model's configuration.

indicates that the pad_token_id is not set in your model's configuration. This attribute is essential for padding sequences during training and evaluation, ensuring that all sequences within a batch are of uniform length.

To resolve this, you'll need to:

    Ensure that your tokenizer has a defined pad_token.
    Set the pad_token_id in your model's configuration to match the tokenizer's pad_token_id.
    Optionally, set the labels_pad_token_id to handle label padding correctly.

Below is a comprehensive guide to help you address this issue effectively.
1. Understand the Importance of pad_token_id

    pad_token_id: This token ID is used to pad sequences to a uniform length within a batch. It ensures that all input sequences are of the same length, which is necessary for efficient batch processing.

    Impact on Training: Without setting the pad_token_id, the model doesn't know which tokens to ignore during loss computation, leading to errors during training.

2. Inspect and Configure the Tokenizer's Special Tokens
2.1. Check if pad_token Exists

First, verify whether your tokenizer already has a pad_token defined.

# Inspect the tokenizer's special tokens
print("Tokenizer's special tokens:", processor.tokenizer.special_tokens_map)

Sample Output:

Tokenizer's special tokens: {'eos_token': '</s>', 'bos_token': '<s>', 'unk_token': '<unk>', 'pad_token': '<pad>'}

2.2. Add a pad_token if Absent

If the pad_token is not defined, you'll need to add one. Here's how:

# Check if pad_token is defined
if processor.tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    # Add pad_token
    processor.tokenizer.add_special_tokens({'pad_token': '<PAD>'})
    print("Added pad_token:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)
    
    # Resize the model's embeddings to accommodate the new pad_token
    model.resize_token_embeddings(len(processor.tokenizer))
else:
    print("pad_token already defined as:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)

Explanation:

    Adding a pad_token: If absent, you define a new pad token (e.g., <PAD>).
    Resizing Embeddings: Adding a new token requires resizing the model's token embeddings to include the new token.

3. Set the pad_token_id in the Model's Configuration

After ensuring that the tokenizer has a pad_token, set the pad_token_id in the model's configuration.

# Set the pad_token_id in the model's configuration
model.config.pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

# Optionally, set labels_pad_token_id if it's separate (useful for loss computation)
model.config.labels_pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

print(f"pad_token_id set to: {model.config.pad_token_id}")
print(f"labels_pad_token_id set to: {model.config.labels_pad_token_id}")
print(f"Pad token: {processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.pad_token_id)}")

Explanation:

    pad_token_id: Informs the model which token ID is used for padding inputs.
    labels_pad_token_id: Specifically informs the model which token ID is used for padding labels. This ensures that padding tokens are ignored during loss computation.

Sample Output:

pad_token_id set to: 0
labels_pad_token_id set to: 0
Pad token: <pad>

(Note: The actual pad_token_id value may vary based on your tokenizer's configuration.)
4. Update the Preprocessing Function (If Necessary)

Ensure that your preprocessing function handles padding correctly. Since you're already replacing pad tokens in labels with -100, it should work fine. Here's a recap:

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

Key Points:

    Image Processing: Converts images to RGB and processes them using the TrOCRProcessor.
    Label Tokenization: Tokenizes labels with padding and truncation to a maximum length.
    Handling Padding in Labels: Replaces padding tokens with -100 to ensure they're ignored during loss computation.

5. Initialize the Trainer

After setting up the tokenizer and model configuration, initialize the Seq2SeqTrainer with the updated model.

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

6. Start Training

Now, you can proceed to start the training process without encountering the pad_token_id error.

trainer.train()

Expected Behavior:

    No Errors: Training should initiate without the previous ValueError.
    Logging: Metrics will be logged to the console as per your training arguments.
    Model Saving: Checkpoints will be saved as configured in training_args.

For clarity and completeness, here's the entire workflow with the necessary fixes to set both decoder_start_token_id and pad_token_id.
7.1. Setup the Environment

Ensure all required packages are installed with compatible versions.

7.2. Import Necessary Libraries

import os
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from transformers import (
    TrOCRProcessor,
    VisionEncoderDecoderModel,
    Seq2SeqTrainingArguments,
    Seq2SeqTrainer
)
from datasets import Dataset
import evaluate
import torch
import numpy as np

7.4. Prepare the Dataset

Assuming your directory structure:

/content/
    word_crops/
        img1.png
        img2.png
        ...
    recognized_words_labeled.csv

# Define paths
images_dir = '/content/word_crops'
labels_file = '/content/recognized_words_labeled.csv'

# Load the CSV file
df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Display first few entries to verify
df.head()

7.5. Create a Custom Dataset

# Create full image paths
df['image_path'] = df['crop_filename'].apply(lambda x: os.path.join(images_dir, x))

# Select relevant columns
df = df[['image_path', 'recognized_text']]

# Convert pandas DataFrame to Hugging Face Dataset
dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(df)

# Display a sample
print(dataset[0])

Sample Output:

{'image_path': '/content/word_crops/img1.png', 'recognized_text': 'hello'}

7.6. Initialize Processor and Model

from transformers import logging

# Suppress warnings and info logs
logging.set_verbosity_error()

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

7.7. Define Preprocessing Function

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

7.8. Apply Preprocessing

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(
    preprocess_function,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"]
)

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

Sample Output:

{'pixel_values': tensor([...]), 'labels': tensor([...])}

7.9. Split the Dataset

# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

Sample Output:

Number of training samples: 900
Number of validation samples: 100

(Adjust based on your actual dataset size.)
7.10. Define Training Arguments

Important: Disable wandb logging by setting report_to=["none"].

training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
    report_to=["none"],  # Disable all logging integrations, including wandb
)

7.11. Define Evaluation Metric

# Load WER metric using the 'evaluate' library
wer_metric = evaluate.load("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

7.12. Set decoder_start_token_id and pad_token_id

This is the crucial step to fix the ValueError.

# Check if decoder_start_token_id is set; if not, set it to eos_token_id
if model.config.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
else:
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id is already set to: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
    
# Check if pad_token is defined; if not, add one
if processor.tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    processor.tokenizer.add_special_tokens({'pad_token': '<PAD>'})
    print("Added pad_token:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)
    model.resize_token_embeddings(len(processor.tokenizer))
else:
    print("pad_token already defined as:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)
    
# Set the pad_token_id in the model's configuration
model.config.pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

# Optionally, set labels_pad_token_id if it's separate (useful for loss computation)
model.config.labels_pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

print(f"pad_token_id set to: {model.config.pad_token_id}")
print(f"labels_pad_token_id set to: {model.config.labels_pad_token_id}")
print(f"Pad token: {processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.pad_token_id)}")

Explanation:

    Set decoder_start_token_id: Ensures the decoder knows where to start generating text. It's typically set to eos_token_id.
    Ensure pad_token Exists: If absent, adds a new pad token and resizes the model's embeddings accordingly.
    Set pad_token_id and labels_pad_token_id: Aligns the model's configuration with the tokenizer's padding strategy.

Sample Output:

decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: 2
pad_token already defined as: <pad>
pad_token_id set to: 0
labels_pad_token_id set to: 0
Pad token: <pad>

(Note: The actual pad_token_id and decoder_start_token_id values may vary based on your tokenizer's configuration.)

trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

7.14. Start Training

trainer.train()

8. Complete Consolidated Code

For your convenience, here's the entire workflow consolidated into a single block. Ensure that you execute each section sequentially in your Google Colab notebook.

# Step 1: Install specific transformers version and other required packages
!pip install transformers==4.37.1 datasets==2.10.0 evaluate==0.4.0 jiwer==4.3.1

# Step 2: Import Necessary Libraries
import os
import pandas as pd
from PIL import Image
from transformers import (
    TrOCRProcessor,
    VisionEncoderDecoderModel,
    Seq2SeqTrainingArguments,
    Seq2SeqTrainer
)
from datasets import Dataset
import evaluate
import torch
import numpy as np
import random

# Step 3: Verify GPU Availability
device = 'cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu'
print(f'Using device: {device}')

# Step 4: Prepare the Dataset
# Define paths
images_dir = '/content/word_crops'
labels_file = '/content/recognized_words_labeled.csv'

# Load the CSV file
df = pd.read_csv(labels_file)

# Display first few entries to verify
df.head()

# Step 5: Create a Custom Dataset
# Create full image paths
df['image_path'] = df['crop_filename'].apply(lambda x: os.path.join(images_dir, x))

# Select relevant columns
df = df[['image_path', 'recognized_text']]

# Convert pandas DataFrame to Hugging Face Dataset
dataset = Dataset.from_pandas(df)

# Display a sample
print(dataset[0])

# Step 6: Initialize Processor and Model
from transformers import logging

# Suppress warnings and info logs
logging.set_verbosity_error()

# Load processor and model
processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')
model.to(device)

# Step 7: Define Preprocessing Function
def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

# Step 8: Apply Preprocessing
# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(
    preprocess_function,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"]
)

# Inspect a processed sample
print(processed_dataset[0])

# Step 9: Split the Dataset
# Set a seed for reproducibility
seed = 42
random.seed(seed)
np.random.seed(seed)
torch.manual_seed(seed)

# Split the dataset: 90% training, 10% validation
split_dataset = processed_dataset.train_test_split(test_size=0.1, seed=seed)
train_dataset = split_dataset['train']
eval_dataset = split_dataset['test']

print(f"Number of training samples: {len(train_dataset)}")
print(f"Number of validation samples: {len(eval_dataset)}")

# Step 10: Define Training Arguments
training_args = Seq2SeqTrainingArguments(
    output_dir="./trocr-finetuned",
    evaluation_strategy="steps",
    learning_rate=2e-5,
    per_device_train_batch_size=16,
    per_device_eval_batch_size=16,
    weight_decay=0.01,
    save_total_limit=2,
    num_train_epochs=10,
    predict_with_generate=True,
    logging_steps=100,
    save_steps=500,
    eval_steps=500,
    fp16=True,  # Use mixed precision if supported
    remove_unused_columns=True,
    load_best_model_at_end=True,
    metric_for_best_model="wer",
    report_to=["none"],  # Disable all logging integrations, including wandb
)

# Step 11: Define Evaluation Metric
# Load WER metric using the 'evaluate' library
wer_metric = evaluate.load("wer")

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

# Step 12: Set `decoder_start_token_id` and `pad_token_id`
# Check if decoder_start_token_id is set; if not, set it to eos_token_id
if model.config.decoder_start_token_id is None:
    model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id set to eos_token_id: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
else:
    print(f"decoder_start_token_id is already set to: {model.config.decoder_start_token_id}")
    
# Check if pad_token is defined; if not, add one
if processor.tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    processor.tokenizer.add_special_tokens({'pad_token': '<PAD>'})
    print("Added pad_token:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)
    model.resize_token_embeddings(len(processor.tokenizer))
else:
    print("pad_token already defined as:", processor.tokenizer.pad_token)
    
# Set the pad_token_id in the model's configuration
model.config.pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

# Optionally, set labels_pad_token_id if it's separate (useful for loss computation)
model.config.labels_pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

print(f"pad_token_id set to: {model.config.pad_token_id}")
print(f"labels_pad_token_id set to: {model.config.labels_pad_token_id}")
print(f"Pad token: {processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.pad_token_id)}")

# Step 13: Initialize the Trainer
trainer = Seq2SeqTrainer(
    model=model,
    args=training_args,
    train_dataset=train_dataset,
    eval_dataset=eval_dataset,
    tokenizer=processor,  # Tokenizer is used for generating predictions
    compute_metrics=compute_metrics,
)

# Step 14: Start Training
trainer.train()

# Step 15: Save the Fine-Tuned Model
# Save the model and processor
trainer.save_model("./trocr-finetuned")
processor.save_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")

# Step 16: Testing the Model
# Reload the fine-tuned model and processor
from transformers import TrOCRProcessor, VisionEncoderDecoderModel

processor = TrOCRProcessor.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained("./trocr-finetuned")
model.to(device)

# Function to predict text from image
def predict(image_path):
    image = Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB")
    pixel_values = processor(images=image, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    pixel_values = pixel_values.to(device)
    
    generated_ids = model.generate(pixel_values)
    generated_text = processor.batch_decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True)[0]
    return generated_text

# Test on a few samples from the validation set
sample_indices = random.sample(range(len(eval_dataset)), 5)
for idx in sample_indices:
    sample = eval_dataset[idx]
    image_path = df['image_path'][idx]
    true_text = df['recognized_text'][idx]
    predicted_text = predict(image_path)
    print(f"Image: {image_path}")
    print(f"True Text: {true_text}")
    print(f"Predicted Text: {predicted_text}\n")

# Step 17: Additional Evaluation (Optional)
# Evaluate on the validation set
eval_results = trainer.evaluate()

print(f"Validation WER: {eval_results['wer']}")

Make sure that after adding the pad token, the model's embeddings are resized to accommodate the new token.

10.1. Still Encountering Errors After Following Steps

        ...

10.3. Data Preprocessing Errors

Ensure that all images are correctly loaded and that the labels do not contain unexpected characters or exceed the max_length.

print(f"Maximum label length: {max_label_length}")

Adjust max_length in the tokenizer accordingly if labels exceed the current max_length.

I'm sorry to hear you're still encountering issues during your training process. The error:

ValueError: Make sure to set the decoder_start_token_id attribute of the model's configuration.

indicates that the decoder_start_token_id attribute is not correctly set in your model's configuration. This attribute is crucial for sequence-to-sequence models like TrOCR to know where to begin generating text.

To resolve this, we'll ensure that:

    The tokenizer has a defined pad_token.
    The model's configuration has both decoder_start_token_id and pad_token_id set appropriately.
    All these settings are applied before initializing the Trainer.

Let's go through the steps systematically to fix this issue.
1. Verify and Configure the Tokenizer
1.1. Load Processor and Model

Ensure you're using a compatible version of the transformers library. Given the previous issues, it's best to use the latest stable version where many bugs are fixed.

4. Define Preprocessing Function

This function handles image loading and label tokenization, ensuring correct padding and formatting.

def preprocess_function(examples):
    # Load and preprocess images
    images = [Image.open(image_path).convert("RGB") for image_path in examples['image_path']]
    
    # Process images
    pixel_values = processor(images=images, return_tensors="pt").pixel_values
    
    # Tokenize labels
    labels = processor.tokenizer(
        examples['recognized_text'],
        padding="max_length",
        truncation=True,
        max_length=32
    ).input_ids
    
    # Replace padding with -100 to ignore in loss computation
    labels = [
        [(label if label != processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id else -100) for label in label_seq]
        for label_seq in labels
    ]
    
    # Convert to tensor
    labels = torch.tensor(labels)
    
    return {"pixel_values": pixel_values, "labels": labels}

5. Apply Preprocessing

# Apply the preprocessing
processed_dataset = dataset.map(
    preprocess_function,
    batched=True,
    remove_columns=["image_path", "recognized_text"]
)

# Inspect a processed sample

def compute_metrics(eval_pred):
    predictions, labels = eval_pred
    
    # Decode predictions
    decoded_preds = processor.batch_decode(predictions, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Replace -100 in labels as we can't decode them
    labels = np.where(labels != -100, labels, processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id)
    
    # Decode labels
    decoded_labels = processor.batch_decode(labels, skip_special_tokens=True)
    
    # Compute WER
    wer = wer_metric.compute(predictions=decoded_preds, references=decoded_labels)
    
    return {"wer": wer}

If you've meticulously followed all the steps above and still encounter the ValueError, consider the following:
12.1. Double-Check Configuration Settings

Ensure that both decoder_start_token_id and pad_token_id are correctly set before initializing the Trainer.

print("decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)

Sometimes, reloading the processor and model after setting configurations helps.

# Reload processor and model

model = VisionEncoderDecoderModel.from_pretrained('microsoft/trocr-base-handwritten')

# Set special tokens and configuration again
if processor.tokenizer.pad_token is None:
    processor.tokenizer.add_special_tokens({'pad_token': '<PAD>'})
    model.resize_token_embeddings(len(processor.tokenizer))

model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
model.config.pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id
model.config.labels_pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

    processor.tokenizer.add_special_tokens({'pad_token': '<PAD>'})
    model.resize_token_embeddings(len(processor.tokenizer))

# Set decoder_start_token_id and pad_token_id
model.config.decoder_start_token_id = processor.tokenizer.eos_token_id
model.config.pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id
model.config.labels_pad_token_id = processor.tokenizer.pad_token_id

# Verify
print("decoder_start_token_id:", model.config.decoder_start_token_id)
print("pad_token_id:", model.config.pad_token_id)
print("labels_pad_token_id:", model.config.labels_pad_token_id)
print("Pad token:", processor.tokenizer.decode(model.config.pad_token_id))

labels_pad_token_id: 0
